<ul><li><span class="noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg/15px-DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg.png" decoding="async" width="15" height="16" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg/22px-DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg/30px-DodgerBlue_flag_waving.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="249" data-file-height="268" /></span></span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Portal:Conservatism" title="Portal:Conservatism">Conservatism&#32;portal</a></li>
<li><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="flag" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b9/Flag_of_Australia.svg/16px-Flag_of_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="16" height="8" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b9/Flag_of_Australia.svg/24px-Flag_of_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b9/Flag_of_Australia.svg/32px-Flag_of_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="640" /></span></span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Portal:Australia" title="Portal:Australia">Australia&#32;portal</a></li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Morrison's political views are considered as <a href="/wiki/Conservatism" title="Conservatism">conservative</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> and he is aligned with the <a href="/wiki/Centre_Right_(Liberal_Party_of_Australia)" title="Centre Right (Liberal Party of Australia)">centre-right faction of the Liberal Party</a>, which he led while in government.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_policies">Social policies</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg/220px-75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg/330px-75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg/440px-75th_anniversary_of_the_landing_of_Allied_forces_in_Normandy_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="667" /></a><figcaption>Morrison with <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II" title="Elizabeth II">Queen Elizabeth II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Head_of_the_Commonwealth" title="Head of the Commonwealth">Head of the Commonwealth</a>, and other world leaders in <a href="/wiki/Portsmouth" title="Portsmouth">Portsmouth</a>, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>Morrison has declared himself a proud supporter of the Australian <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Australia" title="Monarchy of Australia">constitutional monarchy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> In January 2021, he commented that he opposes <a href="/wiki/Australia_Day_debate" title="Australia Day debate">changing the date</a> of <a href="/wiki/Australia_Day" title="Australia Day">Australia Day</a> from 26 January, which attracted criticism.<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Morrison strongly opposes <a href="/wiki/Voluntary_euthanasia" title="Voluntary euthanasia">voluntary euthanasia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Assisted_suicide" title="Assisted suicide">assisted suicide</a>, and has stated that he "believes in the sanctity of human life".<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Morrison's views on immigration have been the subject of media attention,<sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> with <i><a href="/wiki/The_Straits_Times" title="The Straits Times">The Straits Times</a></i> describing his stance as "hardline" and "uncompromising".<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> As <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Immigration_and_Border_Protection" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Immigration and Border Protection">Minister for Immigration and Border Protection</a>, he was responsible for implementing <a href="/wiki/Operation_Sovereign_Borders" title="Operation Sovereign Borders">Operation Sovereign Borders</a>, which requires all asylum seekers arriving in Australia via boat to be refused entry and escorted back to the county they came from.<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> In May 2021, the Morrison government passed laws which would allow refugees to be detained for life in <a href="/wiki/Australian_immigration_detention_facilities" title="Australian immigration detention facilities">Australia's immigration detention facilities</a>, despite <a href="/wiki/Indefinite_detention" title="Indefinite detention">indefinite detention</a> being illegal under international law.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Morrison was an opponent of legalising <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Australia" title="Same-sex marriage in Australia">same-sex marriage in Australia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Marriage_Law_Postal_Survey" title="Australian Marriage Law Postal Survey">Australian Marriage Law Postal Survey</a>, he proposed an amendment to the <i><a href="/wiki/Marriage_Amendment_(Definition_and_Religious_Freedoms)_Act_2017" title="Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Act 2017">Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Bill 2017</a></i> allowing parents to remove children from classes if "non-traditional" marriage is discussed.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> All amendments failed,<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> and Morrison abstained from voting on the final bill.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup> Morrison's electorate of Cook had a participation rate of 82.22% in the Marriage Law Postal Survey, and 55.04% of those had responded "Yes."<sup id="cite_ref-ABS_results_277-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABS_results-277">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> By November 2017, Morrison considered the topic to be a "done deal" and a "finished debate",<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> and same-sex marriage ultimately came into law on 9 December of that year.<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Morrison has indicated support for excluding <a href="/wiki/Trans_woman" title="Trans woman">transgender women</a> from playing "single-sex sports".<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_policies">Environmental policies</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1062260506"><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:40%; ;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>This is coal. Don't be afraid. Don't be scared. It won't hurt you.
</p><p>It's coal. It was dug up by men and women who work and live in the electorates of those who sit opposite—from the Hunter Valley, as the member for Hunter would know. It's coal that's ensured for over 100 years that Australia has enjoyed an energy-competitive advantage that has delivered prosperity to Australian businesses and has ensured that Australian industry has been able to remain competitive in a global market.
</p><p>Those opposite have an ideological, pathological fear of coal. There's no word for 'coalophobia' officially, but that's the malady that afflicts those opposite. It's that malady that's affecting the jobs in the towns and the industries and, indeed, in this country because of the pathological, ideological opposition to coal being an important part of our sustainable and more certain energy future.
</p>
</blockquote>
<p><cite class="left-aligned" style="">—Scott Morrison,&#32;(House of Representatives, 9 February 2017)<sup id="cite_ref-Hansard_282-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hansard-282">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup></cite>
</p>
</div>
<p>Morrison's policies and views on <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Australia" title="Climate change in Australia">climate change</a> have been a subject of interest.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-abc.net.au_283-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abc.net.au-283">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> Morrison, along with the rest of the Coalition, voted to abolish <a href="/wiki/Carbon_pricing_in_Australia" title="Carbon pricing in Australia">Australia's carbon pricing scheme</a> in July 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-285" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-285">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> Morrison also famously presented a lump of coal to Parliament during <a href="/wiki/Question_time" title="Question time">question time</a> in February 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> During his term as prime minister, the 2020 <a href="/wiki/Climate_Change_Performance_Index" title="Climate Change Performance Index">Climate Change Performance Index</a> ranked Australia in last place for its climate policies<sup id="cite_ref-:0_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> and was the only country to score 0 for the same metric in 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_288-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-288">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Australian_bushfire_season" title="2019–20 Australian bushfire season">2019–20 Australian bushfire season</a>, Morrison dismissed suggestions of a link between Australia's emissions or policies and the intensity of the bushfires and initially downplayed the influence of <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_and_wildfires" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate change and wildfires">climate change on the fires</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup> but later admitted that climate change may have contributed.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_133-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Protests over his government's climate policies took place across Australia amidst the fire season.<sup id="cite_ref-290" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-290">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following his attendance of the <a href="/wiki/2021_Leaders%27_Climate_Summit" class="mw-redirect" title="2021 Leaders&#39; Climate Summit">2021 Leaders' Climate Summit</a>, Morrison declined to set <a href="/wiki/Net-zero_emissions" class="mw-redirect" title="Net-zero emissions">net-zero emissions</a> or other climate change targets, unlike other world leaders.<sup id="cite_ref-291" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-291">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-abc.net.au_283-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abc.net.au-283">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> Morrison allegedly requested climate change policy targets be removed from a proposed 2021 <a href="/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_relations" title="Australia–United Kingdom relations">Australia–United Kingdom trade deal</a><sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> and initially suggested he would not attend the <a href="/wiki/2021_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference" title="2021 United Nations Climate Change Conference">2021 United Nations Climate Change Conference</a>, but later confirmed that he would.<sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup> Following the conference, Morrison's government pledged that Australia would aim to achieve <a href="/wiki/Net_zero" class="mw-redirect" title="Net zero">net zero</a> emissions by 2050, but did not introduce this into national law; Morrison said he believed market forces rather than government regulation could address climate change.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_288-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-288">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His government's climate action plan was criticised by journalist <a href="/wiki/Phil_Coorey" title="Phil Coorey">Phil Coorey</a> as "lightweight",<sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup> and by a <a href="/wiki/Climate_Council" title="Climate Council">Climate Council</a> spokesman as "meaningless without strong action this decade".<sup id="cite_ref-296" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-296">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg/220px-Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="308" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg/330px-Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg/440px-Scott_and_Jenny_Morrison.jpg 2x" data-file-width="973" data-file-height="1362" /></a><figcaption>Scott and <a href="/wiki/Jenny_Morrison" title="Jenny Morrison">Jenny Morrison</a> in March 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>Morrison is a fan of <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">rugby union</a> and supported the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Suburbs_RUFC" title="Eastern Suburbs RUFC">Eastern Suburbs RUFC</a> during his childhood. After moving to the <a href="/wiki/Sutherland_Shire" title="Sutherland Shire">Sutherland Shire</a>, he became a fan of the <a href="/wiki/Cronulla-Sutherland_Sharks" title="Cronulla-Sutherland Sharks">Cronulla-Sutherland Sharks</a> rugby league team and was named the club's <a href="/wiki/Number-one_ticket_holder" title="Number-one ticket holder">number-one ticket holder</a> in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-rise_25-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rise-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-297" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-297">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage_and_children">Marriage and children</span></h3>
<p>Morrison began dating <a href="/wiki/Jenny_Morrison" title="Jenny Morrison">Jenny Warren</a> when they were both 16.<sup id="cite_ref-DT_Faith_298-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DT_Faith-298">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> They married in 1990,<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-300" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-300">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-301" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-301">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup> when Morrison was 21 and Warren was 22, and have two daughters together. After multiple unsuccessful <a href="/wiki/In_vitro_fertilisation" title="In vitro fertilisation">IVF</a> treatments over a period of 14 years, their daughters were conceived naturally.<sup id="cite_ref-DT_Faith_298-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DT_Faith-298">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> Their daughters attend an <a href="/wiki/Independent_school#Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Independent school">independent</a> <a href="/wiki/Baptists" title="Baptists">Baptist</a> school. Morrison has stated that one of the reasons for this choice was so that he could avoid "the values of others being imposed on my children."<sup id="cite_ref-302" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-302">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_beliefs">Religious beliefs</span></h3>
<p>Morrison was raised in the <a href="/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_of_Australia" title="Presbyterian Church of Australia">Presbyterian Church of Australia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup> which partly merged into the <a href="/wiki/Uniting_Church_in_Australia" title="Uniting Church in Australia">Uniting Church</a> when he was a child. He later became a <a href="/wiki/Pentecostalism" title="Pentecostalism">Pentecostal</a> and now attends the <a href="/wiki/Horizon_Church" title="Horizon Church">Horizon Church</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-304" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-304">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> which is affiliated with the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Christian_Churches" title="Australian Christian Churches">Australian Christian Churches</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bryant_21-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bryant-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Morrison is Australia's first Pentecostal prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> As a Pentecostal and evangelical church, Horizon is not a mainstream church in Australia. Some members believe in <a href="/wiki/Divine_healing" class="mw-redirect" title="Divine healing">divine healing</a>, and practise "<a href="/wiki/Speaking_in_tongues" title="Speaking in tongues">speaking in tongues</a>", which is seen as a miraculous gift from God.<sup id="cite_ref-molloy2018_306-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-molloy2018-306">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Christian_Lobby" title="Australian Christian Lobby">Australian Christian Lobby</a> welcomed the appointment of a prime minister with such a deep faith, some Australians have been suspicious of its effect on his rulings. As <a href="/wiki/Treasurer_of_Australia" title="Treasurer of Australia">Treasurer of Australia</a> during the vote for <a href="/wiki/Marriage_Amendment_(Definition_and_Religious_Freedoms)_Act_2017" title="Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Act 2017">legislation on same-sex marriage</a> in 2017, Morrison abstained from voting due to his faith.<sup id="cite_ref-molloy2018_306-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-molloy2018-306">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> He has said, "the <a href="/wiki/Bible" title="Bible">Bible</a> is not a policy handbook, and I get very worried when people try to treat it like one".<sup id="cite_ref-bryant_21-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bryant-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> In late 2017, Morrison stated that he would become a stronger advocate for protections for religious freedom.<sup id="cite_ref-307" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-307">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He thinks misuse of social media is the work of "the evil one" and practises the Christian tradition of the "<a href="/wiki/Laying_on_of_hands" title="Laying on of hands">laying on of hands</a>" while working. He said in a speech to the Australian Christian Churches conference in April 2021 that he believes he was elected to do God's work,<sup id="cite_ref-Martin_2021_308-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Martin_2021-308">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup> although he later said that his comments were mischaracterised and that they were meant to reflect his belief that "whatever you do every day... is part of your Christian service".<sup id="cite_ref-309" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-309">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In July 2022, he told a Pentecostal church congregation that, speaking from experience, he believed that people should not trust governments or the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> but should put their trust in God.<sup id="cite_ref-310" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-310">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup> Morrison's successor as prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Albanese" title="Anthony Albanese">Anthony Albanese</a>, found this statement "quite astonishing" and the reference to the United Nations a "nonsense throwaway conspiracy line".<sup id="cite_ref-311" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-311">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1011085734"><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-160"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-160">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In Australian English, "ordinary" can mean bad or undesirable.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-162"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-162">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The statement says that the appointments were made "consistently with" <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Australia" title="Constitution of Australia">Constitution</a> section 64. Section 64 provides for appointment of "Ministers of State" to "administer such departments of State of the Commonwealth as the Governor-General in Council may establish". The Governor-General does this formally on the advice of the Executive Council and in effect on that of the prime minister.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
